## 1. Background information on the procedure

## 1.1. Type II variation

Pursuant to Article 16 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 1234/2008, Janssen-Cilag International NV submitted to the European Medicines Agency on 9 March 2026 an application for a variation.

The following changes were proposed:

| Variation(s) requested   | Variation(s) requested                                                             | Type              |
|--------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.6.a                    | C.6.a) Addition of a new therapeutic indication or modification of an approved one | Variation type II |

Extension of indication to include treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy, for TECVAYLI as monotherapy, based on interim analysis data from the pivotal study 64007957MMY3006 (MajesTEC-9). This is a Phase 3 randomized study comparing teclistamab monotherapy versus pomalidomide, bortezomib, dexamethasone (PVd) or carfilzomib, dexamethasone (Kd) in participants with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy, including an antiCD38 monoclonal antibody and lenalidomide. As a consequence, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.5, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2 and 6.6 of the SmPC are updated. The labelling and package leaflet are updated accordingly. The RMP version 6.2 has also been submitted. In addition, the MAH took the opportunity to introduce editorial changes to the PI and to update the list of local representatives in the package leaflet.

## Information on paediatric requirements

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No 1901/2006, the application included an EMA Decision(s) P/0044/2020 on the granting of a product-specific waiver.

## Information relating to orphan market exclusivity

## Similarity

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No. 141/2000 and Article 3 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 847/2000, the application included a critical report addressing the possible similarity with authorised orphan medicinal products.

## Protocol assistance

The MAH received protocol assistance from the CHMP on 21 July 2022 (EMA/SA/0000091193). The protocol assistance pertained to clinical aspects of the dossier.

## 1.2. Steps taken for the assessment of the product

## 2. Scientific discussion

## 2.1. Introduction

## 2.1.1. Problem statement

## Disease or condition

The MAH submitted an extension of indication application to add a new indication of teclistamab solution for injection as monotherapy for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy.

This application is based upon the interim PFS results of the pivotal phase 3 study MajesTEC-9 (64007957MMY3006).

## Epidemiology

Multiple myeloma (MM) is a rare and incurable plasma cell neoplasm which typically affects adults mostly over 60 years of age. The median age at diagnosis is 65-70 years; MM is very rare in patients younger than 40 years old (2% of cases).

MM accounts for 1%-1.8% of all cancers and is the second most common haematological malignancy (after non-Hodgkin's lymphoma [NHL]) with an estimated incidence in Europe of 4.5-6/100 000/year,

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

with approximately 176.404 new MM cases and 117,077 deaths due to MM anticipated in 2020 worldwide (The Global Cancer Observatory 2020).

## Biologic features

MM is characterised by marrow plasmacytomas (plasma cell tumours) and overproduction of monoclonal immunoglobulins (IgG, IgA, IgD or IgE) or Bence-Jones protein (monoclonal K or h light chains), while the production of normal immunoglobulin is impaired. The cause of a myeloma cell's failure to differentiate is unknown.

MM represents the far end of the spectrum of B cell-derived neoplasms. It is the neoplastic counterpart of terminally differentiated immunoglobulin-producing, long-lived plasma cells (PCs). Long-lived PCs are a subset of PCs characterized by long-term (months to years) survival within the bone marrow (BM) and thought to be key for immunologic memory. Based on karyotype, MM is classified as nonhyperdiploid and hyper diploid, with the latter accounting for 50% to 60% of cases and characterized by trisomies in odd chromosomes. MM has a heterogeneous progression pathway, with multiple relapses over time, whereby several MM cell subclones coexist at baseline and compete for dominance over time, leading to the evolution of drug-resistance clones [Laubach, 2014].

## Clinical presentation, diagnosis and stage/prognosis

The clinical features of MM are varied and can arise from the effects of the tumour itself, or the toxicity of the tumour products, or the host's own immune response.

The most common symptoms include persistent skeletal pain (especially pain in the back or thorax), pathological fractures and vertebral collapse, anaemia, renal impairment, hypercalcaemia and recurrent or persistent bacterial infections. Approximately 20% of patients are asymptomatic at the time of diagnosis.

The most common criteria used in diagnosis of symptomatic MM is the presence of neoplastic plasma cells comprising greater than 10% of BM cells or presence of a plasmacytoma; paraprotein (Mprotein) in the serum and/or urine; and evidence of related organ or tissue impairment due to plasma cell disorder.

The International Staging System (ISS) is used for prognosis. It was revised by the International Myeloma Working Group (IMWG) including cytogenetics by fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) and lactate dehydrogenase (LD), to the Revised International Staging System for Multiple Myeloma, (R-ISS), which is now widely accepted. At the time of diagnosis, patients are typically categorized according to R-ISS, their age, comorbidity and their suitability for intensive treatment.

Despite advances in therapy, MM remains incurable. All patients eventually relapse and with each successive relapse, the chance of response and duration of response typically decreases and ultimately the disease becomes refractory and results in cumulative end organ damage (e.g., renal damage, cytopenia, infections and bone weakening). Patients with relapsed disease can achieve responses to subsequent anti-myeloma regimens, but the duration of response typically decreases with successive relapses until disease becomes resistant to all different options.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Management

Currently there are several different classes of approved agents for MM, including proteasome inhibitors (PIs), immunomodulatory agents, monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) targeting a range of antigens, steroids, alkylators, and selective inhibitors of nuclear export, which can be combined in doublet, triplet, or even quadruplet regimens and used with or without high-dose chemotherapy rescued by autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT) (Moreau, 2021).

Different combination regimens have been studied in clinical trials in these settings (Mikhael, 2019; Dimopoulos, 2021; Moreau, 2021; NCCN, 2022). Despite this, there remains a need for expanding the therapeutic armamentarium with more treatment options, especially for the increasing number of patients who have received 1 or more prior line/s of therapy and are exposed or refractory to standard agents like PIs, lenalidomide, and anti-CD38 antibodies.

Further advanced targeted therapies like chimeric antigen receptor-T cell (CAR-T) and bispecific antibody-based therapies have also been added to the therapeutic armamentarium in recent years. CAR-T therapies are now approved as second- and third-line treatments. Several bispecifics are currently approved for patients who have received 4 or more lines of therapy.

Treatment of RRMM is complex, as it has to be individualised based on several patient-related (e.g., age, ECOG performance status, comorbidities, patient preference), treatment-related (e.g., prior exposure and refractoriness to specific therapies, depth and duration of response [DoR] to prior therapies, toxicity from prior therapies), and disease related factors (e.g., cytogenetic risk status, presence of extramedullary disease [EMD], relapse characteristics [biochemical vs. clinical]).

## 2.1.2. About the product

Teclistamab (INN) active substance is a humanised immunoglobulin G4 (IgG4) bispecific antibody against B-cell maturation antigen (BCMA) and cluster of differentiation 3 (CD3) receptors. The BCMA targeting arm engages a BCMA presenting malignant B cell, followed by the engagement of an activated T cell by the CD3 binding arm, resulting in malignant cell death due to cell lysis mediated by secreted perforin and various granzymes stored in the secretory vesicles of cytotoxic T cells.

Teclistamab is approved:

- in combination with daratumumab for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least one prior therapy.
- as monotherapy in subjects with relapsed and refractory multiple myeloma who have received at least three prior therapies, including an immunomodulatory agent, a proteasome inhibitor, and an anti-CD38 antibody and have demonstrated disease progression on the last therapy.

## 2.1.3. The development programme/compliance with CHMP guidance/scientific advice

The MAH received scientific advice (EMA/SA/0000091193) on the following clinical aspects:

- The proposed fixed dosing regimen for the proposed clinical study and potential extension of indication

## 2.2. Non-clinical aspects

No new non-clinical data have been submitted in this application, which was considered acceptable by the CHMP.

## 2.2.1. Ecotoxicity/environmental risk assessment

Teclistamab is a monoclonal antibody and consequently classified as a naturally occurring substance. In accordance with the Guideline on the Environmental Risk Assessment of Medicinal Products for Human Use (EMEA/CHMP/SWP/4447/00 Revision 1), the MAH submitted a justification for not submitting ERA studies and it was agreed that teclistamab is not expected to pose a risk to the environment.

## 2.2.2. Conclusion on the non-clinical aspects

The CHMP agreed with the MAH's justification for not submitting ERA studies as teclistamab is not expected to pose a risk to the environment.

## Population PK analysis

## Objectives

The objectives of the popPK analysis were to:

- Confirm whether the existing popPK model developed in participants with RRMM from MajesTEC-1 who have received at least 3 prior therapies was adequate to describe teclistamab PK observed in participants with RRMM who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy from MajesTEC-9.
- Assess the effect of covariates on the PK of teclistamab in participants with RRMM in MajesTEC-9.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Derive individual teclistamab exposure metrics based on the popPK model to use as input for E-R analysis.

## Model development

The existing teclistamab popPK model was built based on data from MajesTEC-1 in participants with RRMM. The popPK model comprised a 2-compartment model with first-order absorption and parallel time-independent (representing the nonspecific clearance for IgG) and time-dependent (representing changes in the capacity of the target-mediated clearance) elimination pathways. The model was parameterised in terms of CL1, CLt, V1, KDES, Q, V2, Ka, and F. The parameters and covariate-PK relationships are shown in Table 2.

The existing teclistamab popPK model was fitted to the teclistamab PK data from MajesTEC-9 participants using the maximum a posteriori (MAP) approach. This approach aimed to validate that the existing popPK model adequately described PK in MajesTEC-9 participants. Performance of the model was evaluated using goodness of fit (GOF) plots and prediction-corrected visual predictive checks(pcVPC). For pcVPC, 500 replicates were performed and summary statistics (i.e., median,

## PopPK analysis and external validation

Based on the GOF plots (Figure 2), the observed versus population- and individual-predicted teclistamab concentrations aligned along the line of identity, indicating the absence of significant bias. The pcVPC plot (Figure 3) showed that the model generally captured the central tendency of the teclistamab concentration-time profiles, although the predicted 50 th percentile was slightly above the observed. In addition, the variability of individual concentrations (5th and 95th percentiles) was overpredicted by the model in the new dataset. The MAH concluded that the existing popPK model sufficiently captured the teclistamab PK profile in participants with RRMM from MajesTEC-9 and therefore the existing popPK model was not updated and the existing popPK model is considered suitable for simulations to derive the individual exposure metrics.

## Subgroup Analysis of the Exposure Metrics

The impact of the covariates of interest on teclistamab exposure was assessed using forest plots. MajesTEC-9 participants who received at least 1 dose of teclistamab and who had at least 1 quantifiable teclistamab concentration were included in this analysis. The simulated Cave,1st dose (representing early phase of treatment) and Ctrough,C3D1 (comparable to steady-state trough concentrations with 1.5 mg/kg QW dose regimen) were used for the subgroup analysis. It was assumed that all participants received the protocol-specified dose regimen. The subgroup analysis on the simulated Cave,1st dose is shown in Figure 4.

There was no clinically relevant effect of body weight when teclistamab was administered using a weight-based dose regimen, supported by the 90% CI of the geometric mean ratio being within the range of 0.8 to 1.25. Consistent with the observation in the previous analysis for MajesTEC-1, type of myeloma and ISS stage affected teclistamab exposure. Specifically, simulated Cave,1st dose was approximately 28% lower in participants with the IgG type of multiple myeloma compared with those with non-IgG. Simulated Cave,1st dose was approximately 11% and 28% lower in those with ISS Stages II and III, respectively, compared with Stage I. Similar results were observed when comparing Ctrough,C3D1 in subpopulations from MajesTEC-9 (data not shown). The Ctrough,C3D1 in Chinese participants tended to be slightly lower than that in non-Chinese participants in MajesTEC-9.

## PK simulations

## Comparison of simulated PK profiles under protocol-specified dose regimens in MajesTEC-9 and under approved dose regimens

Stochastic model-based simulations of teclistamab concentration-time profiles for MajesTEC-9 participants were conducted, assuming participants received (1) the protocol-specified dose regimen in MajesTEC-9 and (2) the approved teclistamab RP2D regimen. Although the switching time in the approved dose regimen depends on the patient's response, the simulation assumed a fixed time (i.e., Cycle 12 Day 1), which is around the median switch time observed in the MajesTEC-1 Pivotal RP2D population (11.3 months [range: 3.2-29.5 months]). Therefore, the approved dose regimen simulated for this analysis begins with 2 step-up doses (0.06 and 0.3 mg/kg), followed by 1.5 mg/kg weekly dosing until the end of Cycle 11, and then switches to 1.5 mg/kg every 2 weeks dosing from Cycle 12 onward. To preserve covariate representativeness of the target population (body weight, ISS, and type of myeloma), a simulated population (n=1,000) was generated by random sampling from the 288 participants in Arm A (teclistamab) of MajesTEC-9. The simulated maximum, average, and trough concentrations were derived to characterize teclistamab PK following the proposed dose regimen. In addition, the CL and terminal half-life (HL) were also calculated based on this simulation.

Graphical comparison of PK simulations between dose regimens of 1.5 mg/kg QW and 3 mg/kg Q2W and between dose regimens of 1.5 mg/kg Q2W and 3 mg/kg Q4W (Figure 5 and Figure 6 respectively) illustrate that the steady-state exposures between 1.5 mg/kg QW and 3 mg/kg Q2W and between 1.5 mg/kg Q2W and 3 mg/kg Q4W were generally comparable, although the trough concentrations were slightly lower and the maximum concentrations were slightly higher following the dose regimens with longer dosing intervals relative to those with shorter dosing intervals.

## 2.3.3. Pharmacodynamics

No new data on clinical pharmacodynamics were submitted.

## 2.3.4. PK/PD modelling

## Objectives

The objectives of the exposure-response (E-R) analysis were to confirm and supplement the evidence of efficacy and safety of the teclistamab dose regimen in participants who received teclistamab monotherapy in MajesTEC-9 by:

1. Exploring the E-R relationship between teclistamab exposure and the primary efficacy endpoint PFS.
2. Exploring the E-R relationship between teclistamab exposure and selected safety endpoints, including Grade ≥3 anaemia, neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and infections.

## Exposure-response analysis on efficacy

The relationship between teclistamab exposures (Cave,1st dose, Ctrough,4th dose, Ctrough,1st Q2W, and Ctrough,1st Q4W) and the primary efficacy endpoint, PFS, were explored. The efficacy (and safety) dataset for the E-R analysis included data from a total of 566 participants (teclistamab treatment arm (A): N=283 and PVd/Kd treatment arm (B): N=283).

Most of the investigated covariates were well balanced between the treatment arms and the quartiles of exposure (Cave,1st dose) from Arm A (teclistamab). The type of myeloma showed slight imbalance across the quartiles of exposure in the teclistamab arm. The IgG-type participants were more frequently observed in the lowest exposure quartile (Q1; 81.7%) than in the highest exposure quartile (Q4; 29.6%), indicating that IgG type is associated with lower teclistamab exposure. As expected, the opposite finding was observed for the non-IgG-type participants, in that the proportion of these participants increased with higher teclistamab exposure Q1 (18.3%) to Q4 (70.4%).

Within the teclistamab treatment arm, no statistically significant exposure-PFS relationship was observed for teclistamab, when analysed by quartiles of exposure achieved at the evaluated dose regimen or as a continuous variable in the univariate Cox regression model using exposure metric Cave,1st dose (Figure 8 and Table 5). Univariate and multivariate Cox proportional hazard exposureresponse models for PFS Based on Cave,1st dose for participants in MajesTEC-9 treated with teclistamab in arm A). This finding was consistent across PK exposures measured at longer treatment durations including Ctrough,4th dose, Ctrough,1st Q2W, and Ctrough,1st Q4W (Table 6, Table 7, and Table 8).

The inclusion of type of myeloma (IgG versus non-IgG) did not have a statistically significant effect on PFS (the 95% CI of HR included 1.0, Table 5). Given the small sample size of the ISS Stage III subgroup (n=40), ISS Stages II and III were combined for the multivariate analysis. Including ISS stage (II/III versus I) in the model using exposure metric Cave,1st dose showed a borderline effect on PFS (Table 5), which was not statistically significant in models using exposure metrics Ctrough,4th dose, Ctrough,1st Q2W, and Ctrough,1st Q4W (Table 6, Table 7, and Table 8). Subsequent analyses demonstrated no meaningful separation of PFS across exposure quartiles in ISS Stage II/III (data not shown).

## Exposure-response analysis on safety

Given the high correlation (r 2 &gt;0.80) between Cmax,1st dose and Cmax,4th dose, Cmax,1st dose was selected to perform the exposure-safety analysis as it represents an early indicator of systemic exposure. Within the range of exposure achieved at the evaluated dose regimen, TEAE incidences (Grade ≥3) stratified by teclistamab exposure quartile are presented in Figure 9. There was no increase in TEAE rates with increasing teclistamab exposure for anaemia, neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and infections (Grade ≥3). The association between exposure and the occurrence of fatal infection (Grade 5) was also explored, and no E-R relationship was observed (Figure 9 and Figure 10).

## 2.3.5. Discussion on clinical pharmacology

## Comparison of observed PK in teclistamab monotherapy studies MajesTEC-1 and MajesTEC-9

Observed predose teclistamab concentrations in teclistamab monotherapy studies MajesTEC-1 and MajesTEC-9 increased similarly over the step-up and up to cycle 3 day 1 when 1.5 mg/kg QW dose regimen was used in both studies. In later time points the predose concentrations were slightly lower in MajesTEC-9 participants, presumably because switching to sparser dosing regimen took place earlier in MajesTEC-9.

## Population PK analysis

The MAH used existing teclistamab popPK model to verify the model predictive performance with new clinical data from study MajesTEC-9. The existing popPK model was built based on data from MajesTEC-1 in participants with RRMM who had previously received at least 3 prior therapies. The population PK data set used now included a total of 1,627 PK samples from 288 PK-evaluable participants with RRMM who had previously received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy. As the proportion of PK concentrations below the LLOQ was low (0.26%) they were excluded from the popPK analysis. This exclusion is acceptable.

The existing teclistamab popPK model was fitted to the teclistamab PK data from MajesTEC-9 participants using the MAP approach. This approach can be considered reasonable for the scope of the current variation application.

GOF plots and pcVPC were used to evaluate the performance of the model. GOF plots were generated via the MAP approach by using the existing popPK model parameters as prior information. GOF plots assessed the concordance between the popPK model predictions and the observed data and various plots of residuals. This approach is considered acceptable.

The observed versus population- and individual-predicted teclistamab concentrations were well along the line of identity, indicating the absence of significant bias. The distribution of conditional weighted residuals (CWRES) values as function of population predictions showed that trendline was slightly below zero for most of the population predictions. In the pcVPC plot, it is seen that median of simulated values is slightly above the observed median. In addition, simulated 5 th and 95 th percentiles seem to overpredict the variability in observed data (5 th and 95 th percentiles). However, these differences between observed and predicted data are unlikely to be clinically meaningful considering the observed efficacy and safety of the MajesTEC-9 study, in which the proposed dose regimen was used.

## Subgroup Analysis of the Exposure Metrics

The impact of the covariates of interest on teclistamab exposure were assessed using forest plots with two exposure metrics: the simulated Cave,1st dose (representing early phase of treatment) and Ctrough,C3D1 (comparable to steady-state trough concentrations with 1.5 mg/kg QW regimen). If the point estimate of the GMR for exposure (Cave,1stdose or Ctrough,Q2W) based on covariate effect remained within the 80 to 125% interval relative to the reference group, it was considered that covariate effect was not clinically meaningful. The choice of exposure metrics and subgroup analysis method can be considered acceptable.

Two parameters that were found to affect both exposure metrics (Cave,1stdose, Ctrough,Q2W) were type of myeloma and ISS stage (III vs I). This is in line with the model; type of myeloma and ISS stage are covariates on CL1 and/or CL2 parameters.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

PK simulations to compare PK profiles under protocol-specified dose regimens in MajesTEC-9 and under approved dose regimens

The MAH conducted stochastic model-based simulations of teclistamab concentration-time profiles for the protocol-specified dose regimen (step-up 0.06 and 0.3 mg/kg in week 1, 1.5 mg/kg QW cycles 1-2, 3 mg/kg Q2W cycles 3-6, 3 mg/kg Q4W cycle 7 and onwards) in MajesTEC-9, and the approved teclistamab RP2D regimen. Simulation results for the two dosing regimens were graphically compared for steady-state parameters Cmax, Ctrough and Cave.

The results show that the steady-state exposure between 1.5 mg/kg QW and 3 mg/kg Q2W and between 1.5 mg/kg Q2W and 3 mg/kg Q4W were generally in the same range. As can be expected, the trough concentrations were slightly lower, and the maximum concentrations were slightly higher for the dose regimens with longer dosing intervals relative to those with shorter dosing intervals. Overall, simulated exposures for the proposed dosing regimen were reasonably close to the currently approved regimen.

## Exposure-efficacy analysis

A total of 566 participants (teclistamab arm: N=283 and PVd/Kd arm: N=283) from MajesTEC-9 were included in the dataset for exposure-efficacy analyses. Almost all the investigated covariates were well balanced between the treatment arms and the quartiles of exposure (Cave,1stdose). The IgGtype participants were more frequently observed in the lowest exposure quartile than in the highest exposure quartile and for the non-IgG type participants the opposite was found, i.e. their proportion was largest in the highest teclistamab exposure quartile and smallest in in the lowest exposure quartile. This trend is consistent with the population PK model where the type of myeloma is a covariate on clearance.

Exposure metrics selected for exposure-efficacy analyses were Cave,1st dose, Ctrough,4th dose, Ctrough,1st Q2W, and Ctrough,1st Q4W. The efficacy endpoint was PFS, which was the primary efficacy endpoint in study MajesTEC-9. These exposure metrics and the efficacy endpoint are acceptable for the exposureefficacy analyses.

No statistically significant exposure-PFS relationship was seen for teclistamab, when analysed by quartiles of exposure at the evaluated dose regimen or as a continuous variable in the univariate Cox regression model using exposure metrics Cave,1st dose , Ctrough,4th dose, Ctrough,1st Q2W, and Ctrough,1st Q4W. These analyses are considered acceptable.

The inclusion of type of myeloma (IgG versus non-IgG) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on PFS. Due to the small sample size of the ISS Stage III subgroup, ISS Stages II and III were combined for the multivariate analysis. The inclusion of ISS stage (II/III versus I) in the model showed a borderline effect on PFS. However, no meaningful association was seen between PFS and teclistamab exposure quartiles in patients with baseline ISS Stage II/III. Furthermore, clinical subgroup analyses showed robust and consistent PFS benefit of teclistamab compared with PVd/Kd in participants with ISS Stage I/II/III (see Clinical efficacy later in this AR). Therefore, the slightly reduced teclistamab exposure in IgG type of myeloma and ISS Stages II/III is not clinically meaningful, and no dose adjustment is needed.

## Exposure-safety analysis

A total of 283 in the teclistamab treatment arm together with 283 participants in the PVd/Kd treatment arm were included in the exposure-safety dataset. Exposure-safety was evaluated using bar plots of selected TEAE rates (Grade ≥3) stratified by teclistamab exposure quartile based on Cmax,1stdose. This method and choice of exposure metrics are considered acceptable.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

There was no increase observed in TEAE rates with increasing teclistamab exposure for anaemia, neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and infections (Grade ≥3). Also, no E-R relationship between exposure and the occurrence of fatal infection (Grade 5) was seen. The conducted exposure-safety analyses are considered acceptable.

## Dose justification

The MAH proposed the dosing regimen for teclistamab monotherapy (step-up 0.06, 0.3 mg/kg and 1.5 mg/kg at days 1, 3 and 5 respectively in week 1, 1.5 mg/kg QW in weeks 2-8, 3 mg/kg Q2W from week 9 onwards or 3 mg/kg Q4W from week 25 for patients achieving at least a very good partial response) that was used in MajesTEC-9 study.

Stochastic model-based simulations were conducted to compare teclistamab concentration-time profiles after the protocol-specified dose regimen and after the approved teclistamab RP2D regimen. Overall, these simulated exposures for the proposed dosing regimen were reasonably close to the currently approved regimen and the new dosing regimen is therefore acceptable.

## 2.3.6. Conclusions on clinical pharmacology

Pharmacokinetics of teclistamab monotherapy in participants with RRMM who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy was evaluated in study 64007957MMY3006 (MajesTEC-9) and with population pharmacokinetic analysis with a model developed previously with data in participants with RRMM from study MajesTEC-1 who have received at least 3 prior therapies. The population PK model was also used to provide exposure metrics for exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety analysis.

The analyses conducted are acceptable and indicate no need for dose adjustment based on evaluated covariates, including age, gender, and ethnicity.

Information regarding pharmacokinetic properties of teclistamab (SmPC section 5.2) is updated based on new PK simulations and dosage for the monotherapy has been revised accordingly (SmPC section 4.2)

## 2.4. Clinical efficacy

## 2.4.1. Dose response study

Not applicable.

## 2.4.2. Main study

MajesTEC-9 (64007957MMY3006): A phase 3 study comparing teclistamab monotherapy versus pomalidomide, bortezomib and dexamethasone (PVd) or carfilzomib and dexamethasone (Kd) in subjects with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma.

## Methods

A diagrammatic representation of the MajesTEC-9 study design is presented in Figure 11.

Figure 11. Schematic Overview of Study

<!-- image -->

Participants received study treatment until confirmed progressive disease death, intolerable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, or end of the study, whichever occurred first.

If study treatment is discontinued prior to the onset of disease progression per International Myeloma Working Group (IMWG) criteria, disease evaluation should continue to be performed until confirmed disease progression, death, start of subsequent antimyeloma therapy (unless disease progression is not confirmed prior to start of such therapy), withdrawal of consent, or end of the study, whichever occurs first.

## Study participants

## Inclusion Criteria (selection)

1. ≥18 years of age (or the legal age of consent in the jurisdiction in which the study is taking place) at the time of informed consent.
2. Documented multiple myeloma as defined by the criteria below:
- a. Multiple myeloma diagnosis according to the IMWG diagnostic criteria
- b. Measurable disease at screening as defined by any of the following:
- Serum M-protein level ≥0.5 g/dL (central laboratory); or
- Urine M-protein level ≥200 mg/24 hours (central laboratory); or
- Serum immunoglobulin free light chain ≥10 mg/dL (central laboratory) and abnormal serum immunoglobulin kappa lambda free light chain ratio.
3. Relapsed or refractory disease as defined below:
- a. Relapsed disease is defined as an initial response to previous treatment, followed by confirmed progressive disease by IMWG criteria &gt;60 days after cessation of treatment.
- b. Refractory disease is defined as failure to achieve a response or confirmed progressive disease by IMWG criteria during previous treatment or ≤60 days after cessation of treatment.
4. Received 1 to 3 prior lines of antimyeloma therapy including a minimum of 2 consecutive cycles of an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody at the approved dosing schedule (or minimum of 6 doses if anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody was only part of a maintenance regimen) in any prior line and 2 consecutive cycles of lenalidomide in any prior line.
4. Documented evidence of progressive disease or failure to achieve a response to last line of therapy based on investigator's determination of response by IMWG criteria.
5. Have an ECOG performance status score of 0 to 2 at screening and prior to the start of administration of study treatment.
6. Adequate clinical laboratory values and organ function during the Screening Phase.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

7. Female subjects must not be pregnant and avoid pregnancy through adequate means.

## Exclusion Criteria (selection)

1. Received any prior BCMA-directed therapy.
2. Contraindications or life-threatening allergies, hypersensitivity, or intolerance to any study drug or its excipients. Additional exclusion criteria pertaining to specific study drugs include:
- a. A participant is not eligible to receive PVd as control therapy if any of the following are present:
- 1) Received prior pomalidomide therapy.
- 2) Does not meet criteria for bortezomib retreatment (failure to achieve at least PR to prior bortezomib treatment, or progression by IMWG criteria on therapy or within 6 months after cessation of prior bortezomib treatment).

3) Contraindications or life-threatening allergies, hypersensitivity, or intolerance to pomalidomide or bortezomib (intolerance defined as prior therapy discontinued due to any AE related to pomalidomide or bortezomib)

4) Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy with pain or Grade ≥2 peripheral neuropathy as defined by NCI-CTCAE

5) Received a strong CYP3A4 inducer (see Section 6.11.3.3) within 5 half-lives prior to randomisation

- b. A participant is not eligible to receive Kd as control therapy if any of the following are present:
- 1) Received prior carfilzomib therapy.
- 2) Uncontrolled hypertension, defined as an average systolic blood pressure &gt;159 mmHg or diastolic blood pressure &gt;99 mmHg despite optimal treatment
- 3) Grade 2 peripheral neuropathy with pain or Grade ≥3 peripheral neuropathy
- 4) Contraindications or life-threatening allergies, hypersensitivity, or intolerance to carfilzomib (intolerance defined as prior therapy discontinued due to any AE related to carfilzomib)
- c. Participants will be excluded for either of the following:
- 1) If they have previously received both carfilzomib AND pomalidomide
- 2) If they have previously received carfilzomib and do not meet criteria for bortezomib retreatment
3. Participants will be excluded if intolerant to dexamethasone.

4. Received the following prior antimyeloma therapy, in the specified time frame prior to randomisation:

- a. Targeted therapy, epigenetic therapy, or treatment with an investigational drug or an invasive Investigational medical device within 21 days or ≥5 half-lives, whichever is less
- b. Investigational vaccine within 4 weeks
- c. Monoclonal antibody therapy within 21 days
- d. Cytotoxic therapy within 21 days
- e. PI therapy within 14 days

- f. IMiD agent therapy within 14 days

g. Radiotherapy within 14 days or focal radiation within 7 days

h. Gene-modified adoptive cell therapy (eg, chimeric antigen receptor modified T cells, NK cells) within 3 months.

i. Plasmapheresis within 28 days

j. Received a maximum cumulative dose of corticosteroids of ≥140 mg of prednisone or equivalent within 14 days

k. Stem cell transplant:

a. An allogeneic stem cell transplant within 6 months before randomisation. Participants who received an allogeneic transplant must be off all immunosuppressive medications for ≥42 days without signs of graft-versus-host disease before randomisation.

b. An autologous stem cell transplant within 12 weeks before randomisation.

6. CNS involvement or clinical signs of meningeal involvement of multiple myeloma.

7. Plasma cell leukemia at the time of screening, Waldenstrom's macroglobulinemia, POEMS syndrome (polyneuropathy, organomegaly, endocrinopathy, M-protein, and skin changes), or primary amyloid light chain amyloidosis.

8. a) Any ongoing myelodysplastic syndrome or B-cell malignancy (other than multiple myeloma).

b) Any history of malignancy, other than multiple myeloma, which is considered at high risk of recurrence requiring systemic therapy.

c) Any active malignancy (ie, progressing or requiring treatment change in the last 24 months) other than multiple myeloma. The only allowed exceptions are malignancies treated within the last 24 months that are considered cured:

- 1) Non-muscle invasive bladder cancer (solitary Ta-PUNLMP or low grade, &lt;3 cm, no CIS)
- 2) Non-melanoma skin cancers treated with curative therapy or localized melanoma treated with curative surgical resection alone
- 3) Non-invasive cervical cancer

4) Breast cancer: adequately treated lobular carcinoma in situ or ductal carcinoma in situ, or history of localized breast cancer (anti-hormonal therapy is permitted)

5) Localized prostate cancer (M0, N0) with a Gleason Score ≤7a, treated locally only (RP/RT/focal treatment)

6) Other malignancy that is considered cured with minimal risk of recurrence in consultation with the sponsor.

9. Stroke, transient ischemic attack, or seizure within 6 months prior to signing ICF.
10. Presence of the following cardiac conditions:
- a. New York Heart Association stage III or IV congestive heart failure
- b. Myocardial infarction or coronary artery bypass graft ≤6 months prior to randomisation

c. History of clinically significant ventricular arrhythmia or unexplained syncope, not believed to be vasovagal in nature or due to dehydration

## Treatments

The study consisted of 3 phases: A Screening Phase, a Treatment Phase (Intervention Phase), and a Follow-up Phase (Postintervention Phase). The Screening Phase was up to 28 days before randomisation.

In the Teclistamab arm, teclistamab was administered sc in a 28-day cycle as follows:

## Teclistamab:

- Cycle1:
- Step-up dose 1 (0.06 mg/kg): Day 1
- Step-up dose 2 (0.3 mg/kg): Day 3
- Treatment Dose (1.5 mg/kg): Days 5, 12 and 19
- Cycle 2: Treatment Dose (1.5 mg/kg): Days 1, 8, 15 and 22
- Cycles 3-6: Treatment Dose (3 mg/kg): Days 1 and 15
- Cycle 7+: Treatment Dose (3 mg/kg):Day 1

In the PVd group, pomalidomide, and DXM were administered in 21-day cycle as follows:

Pomalidomide (4 mg po)

Day 1-21 on each cycle

Bortezomib (sc 1.3 mg/m 2 )

Day 1, 4, 8 and 11

DXM (20 mg po, or 10 mg po for participants &gt;75 years of age) Weekly (Days 1, 8, 15, and 22)

In the Kd group, carfilzomib and DXM were administered in 28-day cycle as follows:

1. Carfilzomib (20/56 mg/m2 Twice Weekly Dosing)

Carfilzomib 20/56 mg/m2 twice weekly for 3 weeks each cycle (first two doses 20 mg/m2)

DXM (20 mg iv or po twice weekly , or 10 mg iv or po twice weekly for participants &gt;75 years of age)

2. Carfilzomib (20/70 mg/m2 weekly Once Weekly Dosing)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Carfilzomib 20/70 mg/m2 weekly for 3 weeks each cycle (first dose 20 mg/m2)

DXM (40 mg iv or po weekly , or 20 mg iv or po weekly for participants &gt;75 years of age)

## Pre-administration Medication

To decrease the risk of infusion-related reactions (IRRs), all subjects received the following medications 1 to 3 hours prior to each study drug administration:

- Dexamethasone 16 mg or equivalent
- Paracetamol (acetaminophen) 650-1000 mg IV or orally (PO).
- An antihistamine: diphenhydramine 25-50 mg IV or PO, or equivalent.

## Objectives

## Primary objective

The primary objective is to determine whether teclistamab will significantly improve PFS compared with investigator's choice of PVd/Kd in participants who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy, including an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody and lenalidomide.

## Secondary objective (selection)

The secondary objectives are:

To determine whether teclistamab will improve clinical outcome as measured by:

- CR or better
- OS
- HRQoL
- Overall Response (PR or better)
- VGPR or better
- Duration of response
- PFS2
- Time to next treatment

Disease evaluations were performed by the central laboratory (M-protein and serum free light chain measurements, and immunofixation determinations in serum and 24-hour urine) during the Treatment Phase Treatment Phase Q28D (±7 days) for Arm A and Kd in Arm B and Q21D (±7 days) for PVd in Arm B, even if a cycle is delayed. Response assessments were based on IMWG criteria (2016).

Response to treatment were determined by the investigator, through use of a validated algorithm, and through use of an IRC.

## Outcomes/endpoints

Primary Endpoint

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

PFS, defined as the time from the date of randomisation to the date of initial documented progression to MM in accordance with the IMWG diagnostic criteria for MM or the date of death, whichever occurred first.

## Key secondary endpoints

- CR rate or better, defined as the proportion of participants with a CR (or better) as defined by the IMWG response criteria during or after the study treatment but prior to the start of subsequent antimyeloma therapy.
- Overall Survival (OS), defined as the time from the date of randomisation to the date of death.
- PROs: The MySIm-Q, PROMIS Short Form v2.0 - Physical Functioning 8c, EORTC-QLQ-C30, PRO-CTCAE, and EQ-5D-5L utility and visual analog scores, and PGI-S. Time to worsening and time to improvement for the PRO scores were measured as the interval from the date of randomisation to the start date of meaningful change. Worsening was defined as an increase in score of at least half a standard deviation (0.325) from baseline values, without subsequent improvement to a score better than this level. The standard deviation (0.65) was calculated from baseline scores, combining both treatment groups.

## Other secondary endpoints

- Overall response (OR), defined as the proportion of participants with a PR or better as defined by the IMWG response criteria
- VGPR or better, defined as the proportion of participants with a VGPR or better as defined by the IMWG response criteria prior to subsequent antimyeloma therapy in accordance with the IMWG
- Duration of response, defined as the time from the date of onset of first response until date of disease progression or death among responders, whichever occurs first.
- PFS2, defined as the time interval between the date of randomisation and date of event, which is defined as progressive disease as assessed by investigator on the first subsequent line of antimyeloma therapy, or death from any cause, whichever occurs first.
- Time to next-line treatment (TTNT) for MM, defined as, the time from randomisation to the start of subsequent antimyeloma treatment.

## Sample size

Approximately 590 participants were to be randomised in a 1:1 ratio to achieve a total of 370 PFS events to provide approximately 85% power, with a two-sided alpha of 0.05, for the primary study objective of the study. This assumed 1) a median PFS of 16.4 months for Arm A and 12 months for Arm B, which translated into a HR of 0.73, and 2) an accrual period of 19 months, and an annual dropout rate of 5%. The sample size also accounted for one interim analysis for PFS.

Long-term survival (i.e., OS) follow-up is set to continue until approximately 408 deaths have been observed, which is expected to be approximately 7 years after the last participant is randomised. Therefore, the study was to have approximately 80% power to detect a 25% reduction in the death

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

rate (HR = 0.75), assuming a median OS of 53 months for Arm A and 40 months for Arm B using a log-rank test with a two-sided alpha of 0.05 with 5 interim analyses and a final analysis for efficacy.

## Randomisation

Central randomisation was implemented in this study. Participants were randomly assigned to 1 of 2 treatment arms in 1:1 ratio based on a computer-generated randomisation schedule and stratified by investigator's choice of PVd or Kd, stage of disease at screening per ISS (I vs II/III), number of prior lines of therapy (1 vs 2 or 3), and prior anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody exposure (yes/no), and balanced by using randomly permuted blocks.

## Blinding (masking)

This was an open-label study.

## Statistical methods

The study estimand and the statistical methods were predefined in the study protocol and refined in a statistical analysis plan (SAP). The original SAP was dated 17 May 2023. The analyses presented here are based on the amendment that was finalised 07 May 2025, before the interim analysis CSR, for which the date of last observation recorded was 13 October 2025 and date of database lock 26 November 2025.

## Analysis of primary endpoint

The primary estimand was defined by the following 5 components:

- Population: participants with RRMM who have received 1 to 3 prior lines of therapy, including an anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody and lenalidomide.
- Variable: PFS.
- Study intervention: teclistamab monotherapy (Arm A) as compared to investigator's choice PVd or Kd (PVd/Kd, Arm B).
- Intercurrent events:
- o Subsequent antimyeloma therapy: Hypothetical Strategy: Participants who receive subsequent antimyeloma therapy prior to disease progression or death will be censored at the last disease assessment prior to subsequent therapy.
- Population-level summary: hazard ratio of Tec (Arm A) vs PVd/Kd (Arm B)

The primary analysis consisted of a 2-sided stratified log-rank test, stratified by investigator's choice of PVd or Kd, stage of disease at screening per ISS (I vs II/III), number of prior lines of therapy (1 vs 2 or 3), and anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody refractory status (yes vs no) for the comparison of the PFS distribution between the treatment arms in the ITT Population. The Kaplan-Meier method was used to estimate the distribution of overall PFS for each arm. The treatment effect (HR) and its 2-sided 95% confidence intervals was estimated using a stratified Cox regression model with treatment as the sole explanatory variable.

## Sensitivity analyses

The following sensitivity and supplementary analyses were conducted:

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- using unstratified log-rank test and unstratified Cox's regression model
- with no censoring after missing two or more consecutive disease evaluation
- with no censoring due to start of subsequent anti-myeloma therapies for participants
- defining PD based on investigator assessment according to the IMWG response criteria
- defining PD based on computerized algorithm according to the IMWG response criteria

## Secondary endpoints

The analyses of binary endpoints (e.g., CR, overall response, and VGPR or better) were conducted using stratified Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test. The Mantel-Haenszel odds ratio was provided as the measure of treatment effect along with its two-sided 95% CI. For time-to-event endpoints (e.g., PFS2, OS, and time of worsening of symptoms, and time to next treatment), similar statistical methods were applied as for PFS. For OS, data were analysed as per treatment policy strategy, use time to death irrespective of switching to subsequent anti-myeloma therapy or not. For time to sustained worsening of PROs, death due to disease progression was considered as worsening. Participants without worsening of PRO were censored at the last assessment date of the MySIm-Q before the start of subsequent therapies. Worsening was defined as an increase in score of at least half a standard deviation from baseline values, without subsequent improvement to a score better than this level. The standard deviation (0.65) was calculated from baseline scores, combining both treatment groups.

For the quantitative PROs, LSMEANS were derived based on the mixed effects model with repeated measures, in which the dependent variable is change from baseline in score, and independent variables are baseline, visit, treatment, visit by treatment interaction, and randomisation stratification factors - investigator's choice (PVd vs Kd), ISS staging (I vs II/III), number of prior lines (1 vs 2/3), and anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody refractory status (yes vs no). Unstructured covariance matrix was assumed. For these analyses only on-treatment data were used. All data from a single site were excluded from the analyses because site staff failed to adhere to protocol requirements.

## Subgroups

At the interim analysis, consistency and homogeneity of treatment effects on PFS and CR or better were evaluated across prespecified subgroups sex, age, race, and baseline status with respect to renal function, ECOG performance score, ISS stage, presence of soft tissue plasmacytomas and percentage of bone marrow plasma cells. Subgroup analyses were also done by investigators choice of PVd or Kd, number of lines of prior therapy, and genetic risk group and by refractoriness to antiCD38 monoclonal antibody (Yes, No) and lenalidomide (Yes, No).

## Control for multiplicity

One interim analysis was planned for the primary endpoint PFS. This interim analysis was to be performed after approximately 278 PFS events (approximately 75% of the total planned PFS events) would be observed. The significance level for the test of superiority with respect to PFS was determined based on the actual number of observed PFS events at the interim analysis, using the O'Brien-Fleming boundaries as implemented by the Lan-DeMets alpha spending method. Assuming 278 PFS events are observed, the alpha to be spent at this interim analysis will be 0.019 (2-sided). An observed 2-sided p-value smaller than this value would represent a statistically significant result with respect to PFS. The final analysis for PFS was to be performed after approximately 370 PFS events are reported.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Analysis of OS was to be conducted up to 6 times. The first two OS interim analyses were to be conducted at the same time as the PFS interim and PFS final analyses. The final OS analysis would define the end of study. The overall Type-1 error rate will be controlled at 5% (two-sided) for OS. The Kim-DeMets alpha spending function (Kim 1987) with parameter=2 will be implemented for OS. The estimated cumulative powers for the 5 interim analyses and the final analysis were 8%, 19%, 39%, 60%, 71%, and 80%, respectively, under the assumption of a hazard ratio of 0.75.

If the primary endpoint PFS was statistically significant, then the following key secondary endpoints ordered below were to be tested sequentially for superiority, each at 0.05 (2-sided) significance level, by using a hierarchical procedure to control familywise Type I error rate at a 2-sided significance level of 0.05 (overall):

- Response of CR or better
- Overall survival
- Time to worsening of symptoms in the MySIm-Q total symptom score

No group sequential testing will be performed for the key secondary endpoints except for OS. At the time of PFS IA, data is expected to have reasonably matured for CR or better and worsening in the MySIm-Q total symptom score. Therefore, statistical testing for these two key secondary endpoints will be performed only once using the data cut of PFS IA.

## Results

## Participant flow

Figure 12. Participant Study Disposition as of the Clinical Cutoff Date 13 October 2025 (Database lock 26 Nov 2025); Study 64007957MMY3006

<!-- image -->

## Recruitment

Study initiation date: 29 March 2023 (date first participant was screened).

Interim completion date: 13 October 2025 (date of last observation recorded as part of the database for interim analysis).

The analyses presented in this report are based on a database lock date of 26 November 2025.

## Conduct of the study

The protocol was amended 5 times.

These protocol amendments included the following notable changes:

- Global Amendment 1 revised dosing of teclistamab to be weight-based per a clinical development decision by the sponsor following an urgent safety measure based on data

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

from participants receiving fixed dose teclistamab in combination with daratumumab in Study 64407564MMY1002 (TriMM-2). No participants were randomised prior to the implementation of Amendment 1.

- Global Amendment 2 updated the dosing schedule for teclistamab monotherapy to require all participants to switch to 3 mg/kg Q4W from Cycle 7, with the option to switch to 3 mg/kg Q4W dosing when in confirmed VGPR or better prior to Cycle 7 per investigator discretion
- Global Amendment 3 aligned the administration schedule of the treatment doses of teclistamab in Cycle 1 with the approved prescribing information.
- Global Amendment 4 added Part 2 of the study: single-arm, non-randomised evaluation of teclistamab monotherapy in which an alternative teclistamab step-up dose was utilized.
- Global Amendment 5 removed Part 2 of the study in all countries/territories except for the US. Part 2 will be conducted according to Global Amendment 4/US-2.

## Protocol deviations

All protocol deviations of eligibility criteria and those deviations that could impact participant safety or the primary endpoint were considered major protocol deviations (Table 9).

Protocol deviations did not have a significant impact on the overall results of the study.

Key: FLC = free light chain; ISS = International Staging System; MM = multiple myeloma

ª Includes subjects without a positive immunofixation but with evidence of free light chain kappa by FLC testing.

b Includes subjects without a positive immunofixation but with evidence of free light chain lambda by FLC testing.

e Measurable disease is defined by any of the following: serum M-protein level ≥0.5 g/dL; or urine M-protein level ≥200 mg/24 hours; or serum immunoglobulin free light chain ≥10 mg/dL and abnormal serum immunoglobulin kappa lambda free light chain ratio.

d ISS staging is derived based on serum β2-microglobulin and albumin.

e R-ISS is derived based on the combination of serum β2-microglobulin, albumin, cytogenetic risk, and lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels.

f A subject may have both extramedullary and bone related plasmacytomas. Percentages calculated with the number of subjects with one or more measurable soft-tissue plasmacytomas in each treatment group as denominator.

g Maximum value from bone marrow biopsy or bone marrow aspirate is selected if both the results are available.

h Cytogenetic risk abnormalities are based on central fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) testing, or local FISH or karyotype testing if central FISH is not available.

Note: Percentages calculated with the number of subjects in each treatment group with available data as denominator.

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary endpoint: PFS by IRC

With a median follow-up of 17.3 months a total of 279 PFS events (teclistamab: 88; control: 191) were observed (Table 15 and Figure 13).

The HR for teclistamab versus weekly Kd was 0.28 (95% CI: 0.21-0.39), and versus twice-weekly Kd was 0.31 (95% CI: 0.22-0.43). When compared with the overall Kd population, the HR was 0.29 (95% CI: 0.22-0.38).

All sensitivity and supplementary analyses showed similar results to the primary PFS analysis (data not shown).

## Key secondary efficacy endpoints

## CR or better

The rate of CR or better based on IRC assessment was statistically significantly higher in the teclistamab group than the control group (65.9% vs 16.8%; odds ratio=10.42 [95% CI: 6.89, 15.76]; p&lt;0.0001; Table 16).

## Overall Survival

As of the clinical cutoff, with a median follow-up of 17.3 months, 60 participants (20.3%) in the teclistamab group vs 88 participants (29.6%) in the control group had died. The HR for the teclistamab group vs the control group was 0.60 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.83); the p-value was 0.0020, crossing the prespecified stopping boundary of 0.0066. Median OS was not reached in either treatment group (Table 17, Figure 15).

Figure 15. Kaplan-Meier Plot for Overall Survival; Intent-to-treat Analysis Set (Study 64007957MMY3006)

<!-- image -->

Key: Tec = teclistamab; PVd = pomalidomide, bortezomib, dexamethasone; Kd = carfilzomib, dexamethasone

Subgroup analyses showed that teclistamab provided a generally consistent OS benefit compared with the control group across prespecified and clinically relevant subgroups (Figure 16). Positive treatment effect (HR&lt;1) was demonstrated in all subgroups evaluated except the standard-risk cytogenetic and bone marrow plasma cells &gt;30% to &lt;60% subgroups, in which the HR estimate was &gt;1 with a 95% CI spanning 1:

- In the standard-risk cytogenetics subgroup, deaths due to TEAEs occurred disproportionally in the teclistamab group vs the control group (10 vs 2, respectively) with 9 of 10 Grade 5 TEAEs in the teclistamab group and both Grade 5 TEAEs in the control group being infections. Given the relatively short duration of follow-up for OS, the difference in the OS event rate in this subgroup may reflect random variation rather than a true treatment effect.
- The reduced size of the subgroup for bone marrow plasma cells &gt;30% to &lt;60% (teclistamab: 36; control: 33) may also reflect random variation and should therefore be interpreted cautiously.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Time to Worsening of Symptoms in the MySIm-Q Total Symptom Score

According to MAH, the MySIm-Q is a disease-specific PRO assessment with established content validity for patients with relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma and newly diagnosed multiple myeloma. Worsening was defined as increase in score by 0.325 from the baseline score with no subsequent improvement above this level. Death due to disease progression was also considered an event.

The median time to worsening in the MySIm-Q total symptom subscale was not reached in the teclistamab group and was 19.5 months (95% CI: 16.1, NE) in the control group (Table 18).

## Other efficacy endpoints

## Overall response

ORR (PR or better) based on IRC assessment was higher in the teclistamab group compared with the control group (84.5% vs 54.2%; odds ratio=4.62). The median time to response was 1.2 months (range: 0.9 to 7.9 months) in the TECVAYLI arm and 1.15 months (range: 0.7 to 11.1 months) in the control arm.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## VGPR or better

The median time to VGPR or better was 2.96 months (range: 1.0 to 17.7 months) in the TECVAYLI arm and 2.46 months (range: 0.7 to 20.9 months) in the control arm. The rate of VGPR or better based on IRC assessment, was higher in the teclistamab group compared with the control group (80.1% vs 36.4%; odds ratio=6.98 [95% CI: 4.80, 10.16]).

## Duration of Response

The median DOR in responders (with a PR or better) based on IRC assessment was not reached in the teclistamab group and was 13.4 months (95% CI: 10.4, 17.3) in the control group. The 12month event-free (teclistamab: 85.5%; control: 54.5%) and 18-month event-free rates (teclistamab: 80.6%; control: 40.1%) were higher in the teclistamab group compared with the control group.

## Time to Next Treatment

The median time to subsequent antimyeloma therapy was not reached in the teclistamab group and was 10.3 months (95% CI: 9.1, 12.0) in the control group (HR=0.22 [95% CI: 0.17, 0.30].

## PFS 2

PFS2 (PFS on next line of therapy) was not reached in either treatment group. As of the clinical cutoff (CCO), fewer PFS2 events were observed in the teclistamab group compared with the control group.

Analysis of PFS2 based on cytogenetic risk at baseline demonstrated an improvement n the teclistamab group vs the control group in participants with high- and standard-risk cytogenetics.

## Ancillary analyses

Not applicable.
